| On the live view, the monitor reads BC Wildfire Service fire points and perimeters from this site's mirror, joined by fire number. The visible live view checks the mirror every fifteen minutes. If the mirror is unavailable on entry, the monitor tries the newest captured fleet day and labels it as a dated sample. Statuses of Out are excluded. Satellite hotspots are NRCan CWFIS last-24-h detections, used only on the live view when its mirror answers. On the live view, available Open-Meteo 850 hPa winds affect transit times and target scoring. The mirror limits wind requests to once an hour. The monitor interpolates its coarse BC grid to route midpoints. Missing or stale wind means still air. Weather data by [link] (CC BY 4.0). Where the BC record supplies an official incident link, the monitor provides it. Exercise fires have no official incident page. Data, licences and notices records the sources and their terms. For a real emergency use the BC Wildfire Service, not this page. | Drop-line targeting scores candidate lines using available wind, satellite detections and community proximity, then favours adjacent lines. The model's city list is the population proxy; parcel-level values-at-risk data would refine it. No line repeats until the plan has been treated once. First-order triage, not fire science. Source selection is a size-weighted choice among definite water bodies meeting the class's area and distance rules, larger water preferred over just-qualifying. Fleet allocation scores eligible fires by size, class fit and source distance. Also computed are routes, phase durations, pump and drag and descent power, nitrogen mass-energy cycling, and throughput. The arithmetic section explains selected formulas. The shared model holds the full configuration. | First | The occurrence needs a ledger entry or a rewording. floatcheck floatcheck | ledger entry or rewording |
